- [2026-10-05] AMEND the signer unit is deliberately excluded from the `make units` set; `make signer-unit` stages it without enabling. Installing a role and enabling a process that holds the Master key are different acts, and the second belongs to the election
- [2026-10-05] FIND a superseded root is *valid on its own* — right node, right key, three real member signatures — so "is this root well-formed" cannot detect that the cluster re-formed. The ledger is the memory: every signature records its `root_id`, and starting against a different root needs `--accept-requalification`. A restart under the same root is not a revert and is not refused
- [2026-10-05] FIX the signer would sign a registry entry filed under a fingerprint that is not the hash of its identity. `authz.evaluate` rejects that as `unknown_fingerprint`, so the signature would have been valid over an authorization no peer accepts — `ok` returned, grant silently did nothing. Found because the test fixture was honest enough to fail
- [2026-10-05] FIX `master-node/manifests/node-identity.json` had drifted to `wireguard_pubkey_b64` while `contracts/identity-schema.json`, the peer manifest, `authz.IDENTITY_FIELDS` and the docs all said `wireguard_public_key_b64`. `fingerprint()` ignores fields outside `IDENTITY_FIELDS`, so this raised and warned nothing; the identity just hashed differently from the contract

- [2026-10-05] FIND `opencode agent list` emits ~12 MB on this role, and `subprocess(capture_output=True)` truncated it to ~1 MB — dropping the role's own agents, which sort last. The test read the listing via a pipe first and reported `cluster-admin does not resolve`, pointing at the agent file while the fault was in the test. - [2026-10-05] AMEND two Step 18 assertions were written too broadly and failed on their own explanatory comments: the compose-file scan rejected the state-dir mention in a comment saying it must never be mounted, and the MASTERSPIKE path resolver guessed roots by prefix. - [2026-10-05] INCIDENT during Step 18 mutation M19, `git checkout contracts/ports.json` was used to undo the mutation and reverted the file to the boolean-era HEAD version, silently destroying 5 ports (4200 opencode-serve, 4201 share-intake, 4202 session-transfer, 4203 share-ratification, 4210 cluster-election) and the whole `implementation_state`/`runtime` two-axis rework. The contract tests caught it immediately, and the file was restored byte-for-byte from the last verified bundle. Every one of the 12 original entries now compares equal to that bundle. Two lessons: the mutation runner must save-and-restore the file it mutates like every other one (it saved four others and skipped this one), and an uncommitted contract has no `git checkout` safety net — the bundle is the only copy that survives

- [2026-10-05] STEP Step 19 complete — 617 tests green (355 fabric / 185 peer / 77 master, 1 skip) plus lint, validate, contract-test; `tests/test_election_simulation.py` adds the in-process three-node simulation (elect / refuse / re-form, injected clock, AST-checked for no sleep); Step 19's page now lists the seven things not verified and why; cursor returns to `1` because Steps 1–12 are the next work, not because this plan regressed
- [2026-10-05] FIND `tally()` deduplicated votes by `node_id` and never called `verify_vote()`. A vote is a network packet, so anyone could forge a third ballot naming a member who had not voted and manufacture unanimity — the single property this fabric exists to protect. Untested, because no test had ever forged anything. `tally` now verifies each signature before counting, drops unverifiable votes, and reports them in `rejected`. Mutations M21/M22/M23 all caught
- [2026-10-05] FIND the contract claimed "a new nonce means the old master_pubkey stops being recognised". False: `verify_root` sees one root plus the one already held and cannot order two roots, and a superseded root says nothing about being replaced. Supersession lives in the ledger's `root_id` history and `check_not_reverted`; a peer must *learn* the new root. `contracts/consensus-protocol.md` and `contracts/identity-schema.json` corrected, and the propagation question recorded — a superseded Master must not be able to suppress the news of its own replacement, which is the argument for a lease rather than a broadcast
- [2026-10-05] AMEND `sparknet-bundle.sh` carried a comment saying the Master role "has no code" — true until Step 15, wrong since. Replaced with what is actually true and, more usefully, with what a bundle does *not* carry: the fabric-level `tests/`, so an extracted node cannot run `test_contracts.py` and cannot check contract drift for itself. Parity is proved at the build host before bundling
- [2026-10-05] STEP Step 1 complete — `make keygen` produces three real Ed25519 pairs into `$SPARKNET_STATE_DIR/keys` at 0600 under a 0700 dir, gitignored and untracked; each public half asserted to be derived from its private half and the three to be distinct; `manifests/master-identity.json` describes the role at 10.100.0.1; `contracts/identity-schema.json`'s `master_public_key` no longer claims a tracked key is what peers verify against. 24 tests in `master-node/tests/test_step1_keypair.py`. Mutations M27–M32 caught
- [2026-10-05] FIND Step 1's own DoD contained two contradictions. (a) It asks for `role: master` in `master-identity.json`, but Step 13 replaced that model and `test_a_node_declares_itself_peer_until_it_is_elected` exists to fail exactly that. The cross-role test was right, the DoD was stale; the manifest says `peer` and records the reasoning so nobody "fixes" it back. (b) It asks for a keypair in `$SPARKNET_STATE_DIR/keys` *and* `make keys-check` green, but `keys-check` scanned the vault for key-shaped files and the state dir defaults to `<role>/.state` — inside the vault. Generating the required keypair *failed* the required check
- [2026-10-05] AMEND `keys-check` is now two questions: is anything key-shaped *tracked* (asked of git, since a key deleted from disk but still in history is the failure that matters) and are the state-dir private halves *actually* 0600 under a 0700 dir (since "may exist" is not "is protected"). The old form passed by ignoring exactly the files it existed to police. `test_private_key_is_never_in_the_vault` rewritten to match, plus `test_state_dir_private_keys_are_not_world_readable`
- [2026-10-05] NOTE two mutation attempts (M29, M30) were invalid: they deleted the assertion rather than breaking the condition, so passing proved nothing. Redone as condition breaks (chmod 644; point a public half at a different key) and both caught. Restoring M31 then clobbered the operational private half with the cluster one — caught by the public/private mismatch test, fixed by regenerating that pair
- [2026-10-05] BUG Step 1 silently broke Step 18. Generating the keypair into `$SPARKNET_STATE_DIR` — which defaulted to `<role>/.state`, inside the vault — put `master-node/.state/keys/operational.ed25519` under the admin surface's read-only bind mount (`../..:/workspace/sparknet:ro`). It was readable from the LLM-facing container. Every isolation test passed, because they read the compose file's *text* and the key was reachable through the mount rather than named in it
- [2026-10-05] AMEND `SPARKNET_STATE_DIR` now resolves outside the vault (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/sparknet/master-node`, else `~/.local/state/...`) in `scripts/sparknet-master-env.sh`, with the same default in `services/sparknet-signer.py` and the Makefile's `keys-check`. Existing keys migrated without rotation. Docker cannot exclude a subdirectory from a bind mount, so moving the key was the only fix; gitignore was never the relevant defence. `make keys-check` now fails if the variable is overridden back inside the vault. The peer's `<vault>/.state` default is kept deliberately — no admin surface mounts the peer vault
- [2026-10-05] TEST new `test_admin_surface.py::test_the_compose_mounts_do_not_reach_the_signing_key` resolves every bind-mount source in the compose file and requires the state dir to sit outside all of them — the structural version of the text-based check. Mutations M34–M36 caught. M36 initially survived and exposed a real bug in the new assertion: it tested whether the state dir was an *ancestor* of the mount instead of whether the mount was an ancestor of the state dir. Direction corrected, then caught
- [2026-10-05] TEST `master-node/tests/conftest.py` added so the state dir is resolved once from the env script rather than hardcoded as `<role>/.state` in three test files. - [2026-10-09] PLAN Step 24 **design proposal** — `COMPUTE_CAPABILITY_DESIGN.md` at the fabric root (design-proposal status, mirrors Step 17; binds nothing). Prompted by a 2−2 exercise: the honest finding is that local compute is already free (a peer is authoritative for its own inference), so a `compute` capability gating it would be theatre. The proposal therefore specs `compute-serve` — accept a Master-signed delegated-compute task over the overlay, run it within a closed operation whitelist (`arith`, `digest`), return an attested result — the second `sensitive: true` capability after guard-apply. Specifies the two-place vocabulary change (`contracts/capabilities.json` + hard-coded `KNOWN_CAPABILITIES` in `shared/sparknet-authz.py`, held equal by `tests/test_contracts.py:103`), a new typed signer op `sign-compute-task` modelled on `_sign_guard_approval` (never "sign bytes"), task/result envelopes with params-in-signature and nonce/TTL, ordered fail-closed executor checks (Master source addr → signature → not-revoked → not-replayed → capability → op whitelist → unprivileged → `--execute-enabled`), a new overlay-only `compute-exec` port (guard-9090 reuse and push-receive piggyback rejected), and a worked node131→node133 2−2 round trip. Rejected options recorded (arbitrary code, requester-signed tasks for v1, SSH offload). **Not adopted** — no capability, port, role or contract changed on disk; adoption plan named with live deploy `DoD: confirm: user`. `tests/test_layout.py` `EXPECTED_TOP_LEVEL` registers the new root doc.

- [2026-10-10] Fabric — peer PLAN Step 8 (inter-node sharing and the governance regime) complete — the last step of the peer build-out, and the first cross-node sharing semantics in the fabric. `peer-node/services/sparknet-share-regime.py` is a pure, in-process, message-dispatched state machine (`share.offer/accept/ratify/use/reconcile/close`) that reads the **machine contracts** as its only rule source (`contracts/capabilities.json` share_types + share_clocks, `contracts/overlay.json` governance_mode + data-plane stanza, `contracts/ports.json` share ports) and **refuses to run if they are missing** — so the regime cannot drift from the contract prose, the same anti-drift discipline the guard and the authz ladder use. The four clocks (1 h accept, 20 m idle, 2 h ratification, 10 h absolute) default from the contract and are **clock-injected**, so the DoD that says "these take hours" is tested in seconds. Governance is recomputed from the registry node count on **every** request (never cached): `solo` (drafts allowed, acceptance refused), `peer_pair` (symmetric, both endpoints `flagged`, no ratification — a Master arbitrate is refused `no-arbiter`), `master_governed` (3+) where a Master `ratify` moves both endpoints to `authenticated` with `flagged` retained for audit; a third node joining mid-share **re-evaluates** the live share instead of grandfathering it. Reconciliation for `kb-share`/`full` is additions+conflicts only — a divergent page is a review item, never a silent overwrite — reusing the wiki suite's contradiction detection. Transport is a **time-boxed data-plane stanza** (`render_stanza()`: by-kind port 4201/4202, mtu 1400, keepalive 25, config 0600, single `/32` AllowedIPs, nftables fragment) that leaves the **authority-plane one-address `AllowedIPs` invariant** (`10.100.0.1/32`) intact; non-ratification severs the fabric data plane while peer-to-peer continues, and every share — permanent included — ends at the 10 h cap. The peer bridge's `/session/:id/share` verbs now delegate to the engine (`SHARE_KINDS` is the five contract types), so a share created on the solo development node is an honest **invite** (a 1 h draft), never a false `open`; Step 6's "refuse the fifth kind" test is replaced by "accept all five". `peer-node/manifests/share-regime.json` (schema `sparknet-share-regime/1`, status `wired, not deployed`) declares the types/clocks/actions/authority boundary and a test asserts manifest and engine agree; `peer-node/tests/test_share_regime.py` (26 tests) drives in-process node pairs and a 3-node mesh. - [2026-10-12] PLAN Step 29 **F1 fixed as code** (Phase 5 prerequisite). Recon had shown the bridge's plain handler (`_plain()` → `_loopback_guard()`) refused *every* non-loopback source on the whole plain surface — `/admin/*` included — so a Master dialling the peer's `/admin/*` over the overlay would be refused before the signed ladder; and a naive open would expose the env-profile-injecting OpenAI/session/vault routes. Fix in `peer-node/services/sparknet-dispatch-bridge.py`: new `LOCAL_ONLY_PREFIXES` route class; `_plain()` now applies the shared `check_source_policy(path, source_kind, LOCAL_ONLY_PREFIXES)` so an overlay source gets `403 not-loopback` on the OpenAI/session/vault routes *before the request is read*, while `/admin/*` stays reachable; `--bind` is now repeatable (`action="append"`, default `127.0.0.1`) and `serve()` runs one `ThreadingHTTPServer` per address (sharing one `Bridge`), never a wildcard; `_configure_server()` stamps `loopback_only_routes = LOCAL_ONLY_PREFIXES` on every listener. Two regression tests in `peer-node/tests/test_admin_supervise.py`: a deterministic policy/wiring test and a live `0.0.0.0`-bound test (overlay source → `/v1/models` `403 not-loopback`, `/admin/list_users` passes the source policy; loopback neither refused). - [2026-10-12] **Plan opened: Steps 30–36** — signed-ledger hardening, cross-user proposal/review/countersignature, and the document round-trip transport. A code review found the provenance ledger's block *header* is unsigned and the chain hash unkeyed (F1: `append_block` sets `signature = data.signatures.node`, `block_hash` plain SHA-256, both signatures cover only `data`), so deleting a `grant_use` block drops its nonce from `consumed_grant_nonces` and replays a spent grant; plus truncation (F2), unchecked `block_id` (F3), `genesis_hash` inconsistency (F4), empty-signature acceptance (F5) and no message domain-separation (F6). Gaps: no proposal/review object (G1/G2), `sign_note` refuses a foreign `owner_id` so no countersignature (G3), per-node ledgers never anchor (G4), share regime is data-plane only (G5), transport spec-only (G6). Operator decisions: scope = ledger hardening + proposal/review/countersignature + Merkle transparency log; clean break `schema_version 1.0 → 2.0`; RFC 6962 Merkle as the integrity primitive (signed tree heads + inclusion/consistency proofs) with linear `prev_hash`/`hash` demoted to a local index; build the transport on the existing share ports 4201/4202 as a new share kind, not a new port. Order `30 → 32 → 31 → 35 → 34 → 33 → 36`. Cursor `done → 30`.

- [2026-10-13] PLAN Step 32 **DONE** — RFC 6962 Merkle transparency primitive (Arc C). New `shared/sparknet-merkle.py`: `leaf_hash` (SHA-256(0x00‖entry)), `node_hash`, `merkle_root` (split at largest power of two below n; `MTH({}) = e3b0c442…`), `inclusion_proof`/`verify_inclusion`, `consistency_proof`/`verify_consistency` — generators verbatim RFC 6962 §2.1.1–2.1.2, verifiers verbatim RFC 9162 §2.1.3.2/§2.1.4.2 (statement-by-statement, so a crafted/wrong-length proof is rejected, not normalised), plus `sign_tree_head`/`verify_tree_head`/`tree_head_signable` that delegate to `sparknet-authz.py` by path (`_load_authz`, same loader as provenance) — no Ed25519 of its own. Hex-string leaves decode to their bytes (the published short-leaf spelling and the 64-hex block-hash spelling), odd/whitespace/non-hex refused. Not merely self-consistent: pinned to the canonical transparency-dev/merkle corpus — roots sizes 0..8 (the 7-leaf root is **`ddb89be4…`**, correcting the plan's misremembered `9d56…`), leaf/interior hashes, 7 reference inclusion + 4 consistency proofs, the RFC 9162 §2.1.5 seven-leaf example, and every negative leg the log-verifier `testdata/inclusion/3` + `testdata/consistency/2` JSON corpus names (wrong leaf/root, modified/truncated/garbled/empty proofs, index + size mutation, preceding/trailing garbage) — verified at authoring time against the fetched corpus and re-encoded as in-repo literals. `tests/test_merkle.py`: 46 tests. Receivers persist anchors per sender pubkey in `$SPARKNET_STATE_DIR/anchored-checkpoints.json` (0600, atomic temp+`os.replace`), never in the vault, and refuse fail-closed: a size below an existing anchor is a **rollback**, the same size with a different root is a **fork**, growth demands an RFC 6962 `verify_consistency` proof **and** an advanced head, and a corrupt anchor store raises `ProvenanceError` rather than silently re-anchoring. `_load_merkle` loads `shared/sparknet-merkle.py` by path (same pattern as `_load_authz`). `contracts/tracking-schema.json`: `approval.event_vocabulary.peer` gains `checkpoint` (master unchanged, still disjoint), a new pinned `checkpoint` section, and a `ledger_block.data` note that a `checkpoint` carries no `data.signatures`. `contracts/provenance-protocol.md`: new "Signed checkpoints and cross-node head anchoring" section. Operator decisions: one policy with two allow-lists (`NODE_ROOTS` / `FABRIC_ROOTS`, a strict superset); the stamp refuses on model/sha/digest mismatch and only *reports* a `chroma_version` difference; the stamp lives in `scripts/sparknet-chroma.py`; only the `fabric` scope changes, `peer`/`master` keep whole-vault discovery; the pin follows the design's literal `>=0.5,<1` (resolves to 0.6.3) rather than the current 1.5.9. - [2026-10-22] PLAN Step 63 **DONE** — the substrate half of Step 61's increment 2, with the 4223 service itself untouched (`4223` stays `spec-only`; `contracts/` and `shared/` **zero diff**; the runtime dependency allow-list stays `cryptography` only). Four commits: `f5a45b2` (pin `chromadb>=0.5,<1` + the resolved model on the reindex target), `faa73be` (one corpus policy, two allow-lists — `ROOT_FILES` → `NODE_ROOTS`, `FABRIC_ROOTS` a strict superset, identical exclusions, `--allow-list` on the CLI, the inventory records which list was applied), `78f7afd` (the substrate names the embedder itself — `ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2` = `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, sha256 `913d7300…16ec3` — and passes it on every open; the collection carries `embedding_model`/`embedding_sha256`/`chroma_version`/`corpus_digest`; a mismatch in the first, second or fourth **refuses** with exit 3, the version difference is **reported** only; `make chroma-stamp` makes "reindex on any change" checkable; the `fabric` scope now takes the shared allow-list), `38bd6b5` (the two rough edges the real round trip found: an index **written by another chromadb version is rebuilt, not served** — the version marker is a plain file because the collection metadata cannot be read precisely when the index is unreadable, and the discard happens *before* any client opens the directory because chroma caches its storage system per path; and the index is made **equal** to the corpus, because an upsert leaves the ids that *left* the corpus behind while the digest — which hashes what was written — still matched). **Measured (2026-10-22):** the allow-listed `fabric` corpus is **28 markdown files, 381 KiB, 242 chunks** — about a seventh of the old chunk count, and none of it personal; the deny-list corpus was 138 files / 1,699 chunks. **The numeric trigger** for re-opening Milvus/Qdrant is now written down (design F2): **>100,000 chunks**, or **p95 > 200 ms** over 1,000 queries, or **a third host joining the fabric**; - [2026-10-24] PLAN Step 66 **OPENED — 66A DONE (cross-language contract change).** Operator direction, Phase 2 of the six-phase arc, with two decisions that shape it: a node's department is **attested by the Master in the signed registry**, never self-declared, and crossing a department boundary is **a new capability in the closed vocabulary**. The first is the security decision — in-department sharing is the *easier* path, so a self-declared department would let a node claim its way into whichever department it wanted to read, which is exactly the direction an attacker pushes; attestation therefore belongs where the rest of authority belongs, inside the envelope the Master signs. **66A:** `department` joins `entry_fields` (optional, `["string","null"]`, modelled on `revoked`) and `signed_envelope_fields` in **both** `contracts/registry-schema.json` and `contracts/identity-schema.json`; `cross-department-share` joins `known_capabilities` with a definition; `shared/sparknet-authz.py` gains it in `SIGNED_FIELDS`, in `authorization_message`'s envelope and in `KNOWN_CAPABILITIES`. The envelope carries `department` **always**, as `null` when unattested — one message shape rather than one-per-optional-field, because two canonical forms for the same logical entry is the ambiguity the byte-for-byte rule exists to prevent. **This was not one repo's change.** The envelope is implemented twice: `tests/test_registry_crossverify.py` exists precisely because a DGX Spark node's goSpark agent verifies a registry the fabric's Python signed, and two implementations of one signature agree only if both are edited. goSpark's `spec/sparknet/PROVENANCE.md` states the rule — the Go vocabulary and the snapshot move in the same change or not at all — so both moved: snapshots re-copied verbatim, `CapCrossDepartmentShare` added and marked sensitive (the holder bears the risk), `apiv1.NodeAuthorization.Department` added, `signedEnvelope.Department` added **without `omitempty`** so it serialises as `null` exactly as Python does, and `TestVocabularyMatchesFabric` updated. Golden bytes were changed on both sides only after the delta was shown to be exactly the added `"department":null` — no key removed, no value altered — the same discipline Step 65A used for the cache-key vectors, because regenerating a golden value first and asking why afterwards is how a rename hides a real change. (ii) `item_name` is **inside** the signature, because where an answer lands is part of the authorisation and an unsigned name would let a deliverer choose the destination; it is validated as a single safe component at signing *and again at the write*, since a write guarded by a check somewhere else is a write guarded by nothing once that other code moves. (iii) **No monotonic sequence**, unlike the registry publication: a registry *supersedes*, so a lower sequence is a replay, whereas answers *accumulate*, so the idempotence key is the `request_id` the answer echoes — a re-delivery is `duplicate_answer` rather than a second copy, and replaying an older answer for one request is impossible for the same reason. An **empty answer is legitimate** (`content_b64` is in the envelope but not in `RFI_ANSWER_REQUIRED`) because "nothing found" is an answer, and refusing it would push a Master toward inventing content to fill the field. Installing composes with 67A rather than duplicating it: write the item, record the `request_id`, append an `inbox_acceptance` block with source `master-node`. The item is written **before** the request id is recorded so a crash between them re-delivers rather than loses the answer; the reverse order would leave a ledger claiming a delivery that never happened, which is worse than a duplicate. `verify_rfi_answer` **never raises** for a refusal — a caller that must catch in order to refuse will eventually catch one too few — and a corrupt install record is read as empty rather than becoming a reason to accept a replay. - [2026-10-25] PLAN Step 70 **70E DONE — the ledger head is anchored periodically, idempotently, and the signed checkpoint reaches the Master.** `peer-node/services/sparknet-checkpoint-anchor.py` verifies the chain with the shared provenance module, then appends a checkpoint only when the head has moved. The idempotence rule is the part worth keeping: `build_checkpoint_data` snapshots the chain *before* the checkpoint, so after the first append the checkpoint is itself the newest block and a naive re-run would see `size + 1` forever. The service recognises the current-head case instead — the newest block is a checkpoint whose `data.size` equals the number of blocks before it, whose `head_hash` is that block's hash and whose `root` re-computes over them — and writes nothing; a chain that grew past it gets a new checkpoint. A re-run over an unchanged head therefore leaves the ledger at two blocks (seed + checkpoint) and diverges only by re-pushing the same signed block, which is deliberate evidence refresh, not new state. The payload is the **full signed block** (`{"checkpoint": block}`), not three bare numbers, so the Master can run `provenance.verify_checkpoint` and hold the signature itself. An empty ledger exits 0 with a reason; a tampered chain exits 1 without appending; `--dry-run` prints the planned head and writes nothing. Unit `sparknet-checkpoint-anchor.service` (oneshot) + `.timer` (15 min, persistent) are wired at all three installer points (shim body, user-shim loop, `USER_UNITS`), and the enable hint names the timer. **Contract truth moved with the code:** `telemetry-schema.json` gains a `checkpoint_anchor` implementation-state row and refreshed consumers/honest-pair/kinds text, `provenance-protocol.md` replaces "no new port exists yet" with the built 8090 path and the idempotence rule, and `MASTER_OBSERVABILITY_DESIGN.md` marks 69F "(built as 70E)" and stops claiming nothing calls the builder; `make render` regenerated the manifest. **Tests:** 8 in `tests/test_checkpoint_anchor.py` — a real service subprocess against the real 8090 intake (Merkle root over the chain, signed-block payload, accepted report), an unchanged re-run appending nothing and re-pushing the same hash, empty-chain silence, ungranted anchoring without a push, dry-run no-write, tampered-chain refusal, registry untouched + no-authority scan, and unit/timer/installer/contract pins. Gates: `make render`, `make validate`, `make ci CI_QUICK=1` → all steps passed, `make test-all` EXIT=0 (535 passed / 1 skipped), and `bundle-verify` green for **both** roles (peer `20261025-164614`, master `20261025-164740`). Live cross-node anchoring stays `DoD: confirm: user` (no overlay here). Cursor stays `70`; **70F is next**.

- [2026-10-30] **Registry distribution audit (Step 6, closed 2026-10-24) — four defects found and fixed; no plan step moved.** (1) `contracts/registry-publication.json` was not valid JSON in the worktree (trailing comma after `distribution.bootstrap`), so `tests/test_contracts.py` could not load the publication contract; fixed and re-rendered. (2) `shared/sparknet-registry-install.py` tripped the blocking `SIM115` (no context manager) and `make format-check` reformatted five files; the atomic write now uses a `with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(...)` and the lint/format gates are clean. (3) **The re-delivery timer was set up to fail forever**: the guard refused *any* sequence not greater, so the second 60 s push of the publication a peer had just installed returned `409 stale_publication` and left `sparknet-registry-deliver.service` red on every tick. The rule in `accept_publication` is now: a lower sequence is a rollback (`409`), an equal sequence with the *same* digest is `REGISTRY_ALREADY_INSTALLED` (no write — the timer's steady state), and an equal sequence with a *different* digest is a fork (`409 stale_publication`). The guard logs `registry_unchanged` for the no-op and the offline client prints "already installed". (4) `sparknet-registry-deliver.sh` would POST to an entry at the overlay anchor (`10.100.0.1`), i.e. to the Master itself, where no guard exists; it now skips that address with an explicit `skipped: master_anchor` record instead of failing the unit. Docs/contracts updated with the nuance (`registry-protocol.md`, `registry-publication.json` `sequence_rule`/`stale_publication`/`idempotence`, `shared/README.md`, both USER_MANUALs, master plan Step 6 note, master/root MEMORY, `index.md` contract row restored), `make render` run, two new tests (`test_redelivering_the_same_publication_is_a_no_op`, `test_the_master_anchor_is_not_a_delivery_target`) plus the offline-client replay assertion rewritten to the no-op semantics. Gates: `make ci` **all steps passed** (fabric + contract green; peer 545 passed/1 skipped; master parity+spec green), `make test-all` green, `make lint`/`format-check` clean. No commit made.
